5.4
Invariance in the second coordinate
In this section, we consider an invariance property for χU(x ∣y) with respect to y. We show that
y and w generate the same chronological algebraic closure, then χU(x ∣y) = χU(x ∣w). Here the
chronological algebraic closure is a version of the algebraic closure in model theory [8, §10]. We frame
the definition here in a self-contained way.
Definition 5.20. Let M be an Lproc-structure satisfying Tstat, let m ∈N ∪{∞} and z ∈M0. We say
that z is in the chronological algebraic closure of y ∈Mm
0 , or z ∈aclM
chron(y), if the following conditions
hold:
(1) The set K = {z′ ∈M0 ∶tpM
chron(z′,y) = tpM
chron(z,y)} is compact.
(2) There exists a chronologically definable predicate φ with respect to Tstat such that
d(z′,K) = ψM(z,y) for z ∈M0.
We remark that since every Ltr formula evaluated in M0 is also a chronological formula, the
chronological algebraic closure of y contains the algebraic closure with respect to Ltr and Ttr (see
[45, §3]), and hence in particular contains the von Neumann algebra generated by y. Our goal is to
prove the following monotonicity result. Note that in the case that y0 and w0 generate the same
chronological algebraic closure, then we have inequalities in both directions, hence equality.
Proposition 5.21. Let M be an Lproc-structure satisfying Tstat, let m ∈N and m1,m2 ∈N ∪{∞}.
Let x0 ∈M m
0
and y0 ∈M m1
0
and w0 ∈M m2
0
. Suppose that for each j ∈N, y′
j ∈aclM
chron(y). Then
˜χU
chron(x0 ∣w0) ≥˜χU
chron(x0 ∣y0).
We first prepare two technical lemmas for the proof. The first shows that tuples from the algebraic
closure also live in a compact set K, and that finitely many balls will cover a neighborhood of K
defined by some restricted chronological formula. By expressing this condition in terms of restricted
chronological formulas, we make it easier to translate it to a condition on the microstate spaces to
prove Proposition 5.21.

The next technical lemma shows that deterministic matrix models for y0 can be completed to
deterministic matrix models for (y0,w0), which can be proved using a diagonalization argument.
Later, Theorem 7.9 will prove a much stronger result about completing matrix models of optimal
couplings. Hence, for the sake of time, we will skip giving a self-contained proof for Lemma 5.23
and simply invoke Theorem 7.9. Needless to say, Theorem 7.9 does not depend on the results of this
section in any way.

5.5
Large deviations
We conclude the section with a discussion of large deviations theory. Here it is convenient to work with
a certain compactification of Schron,m(Tstat). For similar compactification constructions in the context
of free entropy, see [11] and [48, §7]. Note Schron,m(Tstat) is not itself compact without imposing a
certain bound on the operator norm, and the large deviations principle can assign weight to regions
that do not necessarily satisfy a fixed operator norm bound.
Recall we defined F
0
chron,m as the
completion of F0
chron,m with respect to the uniform norm associated to Tstat. It is not hard to see that
F
0
chron,m is a commutative real C∗-algebra, and therefore F
0
chron,m is isomorphic to C(Σchron,m;R),
where Σchron,m is its character space or Gelfand spectrum. The type space Schron,m(Tstat) naturally
embeds into Σchron,m by viewing each type as a character on F
0
chron,m through the natural dual pairing.

predicate with no free variables. Thus, the value of P(φ) only depends on the chronological theory of
the ambient Lproc-structure. The definition of entropy χU

by letting

lower semicontinuous on Σchron,m.
The statement (5.14) for the convergence of matrix pressure thus gives an analogous statement
to Varadhan’s lemma with ˜χU

By Varadhan’s lemma and Bryc’s theorem, the Gaussian measures σ(n) and the space Σqf,m

ultrafilters on N.
6
Change of variables for entropy
In this section, we examine the change of variables formula for the free entropy of chronological types.
While we studied entropy ˜χU
chron(x ∣y) with respect to the Gaussian measure in the previous section,
we introduce the corresponding entropy χU
chron(x ∣y) with respect to Lebesgue measure here, including
showing that in Definition 5.1 one gets the same result by restricting to microstate spaces with an
operator-norm bound.
Motivated by the application of geodesic concavity, we focus on extending the change of variables
formula to gradients of functions of significantly less smoothness than has been considered in past
work. Indeed, relating χU
chron(f(x) ∣y) to χU
chron(x ∣y) is clearly possible when f is a power series or
a noncommutative smooth function along similar lines as [70, Proposition 3.5]. However, in studying
transport along a Wasserstein geodesic, it is necessary to study the derivative of χU
chron((id+t∇φ)x ∣y)
when φ is merely a chronologically definable predicate, which we may assume to be semiconvex and
semiconcave. This requires defining a Laplacian ∆φ in much more general setting. As explained in
the introduction, since φ can now be a formula involving suprema and infima, there is no way to give
an explicit formula for the Laplacian as one can do in the setting of trace polynomials. Rather the
Laplacian has to be defined abstractly by the action of the process zs,t in our Lproc-structure.
6.1

an operator-norm cutoff. This result is a variant of standard results that were proved for the original
free entropy [70, Proposition 2.4], [11, Lemma 7.1], [6].

The next proposition is extremely important for the proofs of our main results.
It says that
χU
chron(x ∣y) is the largest possible limit of normalized classical entropies for random matrix models
for x compatible with fixed deterministic models for y. The statement here is a direct adaptation of
[49, Theorem 4.10].

We point out that hypothesis (3) holds for Ginibre random matrices by the Herbst concentration
inequality (2.2). The proof of [49, Theorem 4.10] works almost verbatim, and so we will not repeat
the argument here. We merely comment on the few differences:
• Because we are using non-self-adjoint matrices, the real dimension is twice as large, so the
normalizing constant in h(n) is 2mlog n rather than mlog n.
• In [49, Theorem 4.10], the entropy was defined for a fixed choice of matrix approximations Y(n)
for y. In the setting here, we have already proved that the value is the same for all choices of
Y(n).
• The proof of [49, Theorem 4.10] used microstate spaces associated to general open neighborhoods
in the space of laws. For this proposition, one uses instead the spaces Γ(n)
R (x ∣Y(n) ↝y;Φ,ε).

will be taken over balls in the 0-level of the filtration M0.

6.4
The Laplacian
Definition 6.19. Let m ∈N and m′ ∈N ∪{0,∞}. We define an operation Pm,m′,t0 on formulas in
free variables x = (xj)m
j=1 and y = (yj)m′
j=1 by
(Pt0φ)(x,y) = (St0φ)(x + (z0,t0,j)m
j=1,y).
In other words, we perform the same transformations as St0 and also replace xj with xj + zt0,j. Note
that we have assumed that the variables bound to some quantifier have been disjointified from the
free variables, so in particular, the bound variables are left unchanged by our substitutions. When m
and m′ are clear from context, we will often abbreviate Pm,m′,t to simply Pt.
Remark 6.20. The operation Pt leaves the space of chronological formulas invariant. Moreover, for a
theory T containing Tstat, we have that ∥Ptφ∥0,r,T ≤∥φ∥t,r+6
√
t,T = ∥φ∥0,r+6
√
t,T. Hence, by passing to
the completion, Tt is well-defined on the space of chronologically definable predicates with respect to
T.
Lemma 6.21. Tproc implies that Pt0Pt1φ = Pt0+t1φ for all formulas. Similarly, if T contains Tstat,
then this holds when φ is a chronologically definable predicate.
Proof. Each occurrence of xj in φ is replaced by xj + z0,t1,j in Pt1φ. Then xj + z0,t1,j is replaced by
xj + z0,t0,j + zt0,t0+t1,j in Pt0Pt1φ. Since T0
proc requires that z0,t0,j + zt0,t0+t1,j = z0,t0+t1,j, the overall
effect is the same as if we replaced xj by xj + z0,t0+t1,j in Pt0Pt1φ which is the same as what happens
in Pt0+t1φ. The transformation of the constant symbols zt,j and the quantifiers proceeds in the same
way as for Pt0Pt1.
The second claim follows by applying the first claim to chronological formulas and then passing to
the completion.

7
Entropy and Wasserstein geodesics
In this section, we will study the Wasserstein distance on spaces of chronological types, prove an
analog of Monge–Kantorovich duality for the Wasserstein distance, and prove geodesic concavity and
the evolution variational inequality for χU
chron. In [46], we studied the behavior of entropy for types
in the language of tracial von Neumann algebras, and showed the entropy in that setting is locally
75


## Page 76


Lipschitz along Wasserstein geodesics. The setting of chronological types in this paper makes the
study of entropy more tractable and allows us to obtain geodesic concavity. In fact, this works more
generally for the conditional entropy χU
chron(x ∣y) in x.
This is defined by working over a space of chronological types for x relative to a fixed y. The type
of x over y sends a formula φ in m + m′ variables to φM(x,y). We remark that formulas in x and y
can be viewed as formulas in x in an expanded language with constant symbols added for y, and this
is the usual perspective taken in model theory.
Before proving geodesic concavity, we first describe the Wasserstein distance for the types relative
to y, and generalize the Monge–Kantorovich duality from [45] to this setting. The proof of concavity
combines this optimal transport machinery with the general infinitesimal change of variables for
entropy in Theorem 6.25.
7.1
The space of chronological types over y
Although it is more proper to associate a type space to a theory T rather than a particular structure
(hence the notation Schron,m(T) above), it will be convenient here to work in a (sufficiently large)
fixed structure throughout. This approach will probably be more intuitive to non-model theorists
than fixing another theory T in the language Lchron,y expanded by constant symbols.
Hence, throughout the discussion, we will work with a fixed Lproc-structure M which is countably
saturated.
Countable saturation is a condition that guarantees that if some relations in a count-
able family of variables can be approximately satisfied in M, then they can be exactly satisfied in
M. We remark that ultraproducts usually have countable saturation, which can be proved using a
diagonalization argument. See [45, §2.6].
Definition 7.1. An Lproc-structure M is said to be countably saturated if the following holds: Let
m,m′ ∈N ∪{∞}. Let Φ be a collection of Lproc-formulas in m variables. Let r ∈(0,∞)m and let
y ∈M m′
0 . Suppose that for every finite F ⊆Φ and ε > 0, there exists x ∈∏m
j=1 DM
0,rj such that
max
φ∈F ∣φ(x,y)∣< ε.
Then there exists x ∈∏m
j=1 DM
0,rj such that
∀φ ∈Φ,φM(x,y) = 0.
Remark 7.2. Countable saturation is related to compactness because it is saying that if the collection
of sets {x ∶∣φ(x,y)∣≤ε} for φ ∈Φ and ε > 0 have the finite intersection property, then their total
intersection is nonempty. See also [45, Proposition 2.28].

7.2
Monge–Kantorovich duality
Now we can prove the analog of Monge–Kantorovich duality for the setting of chronological types over
a given y. The theorem and proof are an adaptation of [45, Theorem 1.1], but parts of the argument
are simpler thanks to working with functions which are quadratic plus ∥⋅∥1-Lipschitz. Compare also
[30, Proposition 1.3] for the setting of quantifier-free types over Ltr (noncommutative laws).

7.4
Evolution variational inequality
In this section, we show that the heat evolution in Schron,m,∖(Q/y0) is the gradient flow for entropy
in a suitable sense.
Suppose that x0 ∈Qm
0 with ν0 = tpQ
chron(x0,y) ∈Schron,m,∖(Q/y0). Let zt = (z0,t,j)m
j=1 be the
canonical Brownian motion in Qm, and let νt be the chronological type of (x0 + zt,y0) in the shifted
structure StQ. Note that for a chronological formula φ,
(νt,φ) = φStQ(x0 + zt,y0) = (Ptφ)Q(x0,y0) = (ν0,Ptφ),
where Pt is the heat semigroup with respect to the first m variables. This shows that νt is a continuous
function of ν0 with respect to the logic topology, and in particular, it does not depend on the choice
of representative (x0,y0) of the type ν0. We will thus also denote νt as P †
t ν0. Since Pt is a semigroup
acting on chronological formulas, we see that P †
t is a semigroup acting on chronological types over y0.
